### 14.1 The Bekenstein–Hawking area law

**Theorem 14.1 (Bekenstein–Hawking from Point-density on horizon).** The Bekenstein–Hawking entropy
> S_BH = A / (4 ℓ_P²)

is the count of pointwise McGucken generators ℱ_p on the horizon 2-sphere, with each generator occupying one Planck area ℓ_P² = ℏG/c³ [8, Theorem 22].

*Proof — five steps.*

- **Step 1 (Horizon as 2-sphere):** Event horizon 𝓗 of black hole of Schwarzschild radius r_s = 2GM/c² is a 2-sphere of area A = 4πr_s².

- **Step 2 (Pointwise generators):** Every point of 𝓗 is the seat of a pointwise McGucken Operator ℱ_p [29, Theorem 5.5].

- **Step 3 (Planck-area quantization via three-step Schwarzschild self-consistency):** Following [8, Theorem 11, §11.2]:
- **(i)** McGucken Principle fixes c.
- **(ii)** Action quantization fixes ℏ: one quantum of action per substrate cycle, ℏ = E·λ/c.
- **(iii)** Schwarzschild self-consistency: r_S(E) = λ. Substrate quantum of energy E at wavelength λ has Schwarzschild radius r_S = 2G·E/c⁴ = 2Gℏ/(λc³). Setting r_S = λ: λ² = 2Gℏ/c³, hence:
> **ℓ_*² = ℏG/c³ = ℓ_P²**

The Planck triple (ℓ_P, t_P, ℏ) is the McGucken substrate's internal scale.

- **Step 4 (Operator count):** N(𝓗) = A/ℓ_P². Each generator uniquely determines its Sphere (independence verification via [29, Theorem 5.4]).

- **Step 5 (Quarter-bit factor via McGucken-Wick Euclidean cigar):** The McGucken-Wick substitution τ = x₄/c (from x₄ = ict, the substitution is a *coordinate identification* not an analytic-continuation trick, [14, Theorem 6]). Applying to Schwarzschild:
> ds²_E = (1 − 2GM/c²r) c² dτ² + (1 − 2GM/c²r)⁻¹ dr² + r² dΩ²

Near horizon r → r_s, with proper-radial coordinate ρ:
> ds²_E ≈ (ρ²c²/4r_s²) dτ² + dρ² + r_s² dΩ²

Smoothness at ρ = 0 forces τ-periodicity:
> **τ_period = 4πr_s/c = 8πGM/c³**

Thermal-periodicity identification β = ℏ/(k_B T) gives:
> T_H = ℏc³/(8πGM k_B) — Hawking temperature

Integrating dM = T_H dS:
> S = (4πGM²k_B)/(ℏc³)

Substituting A = 4πr_s² = 16πG²M²/c⁴:
> **S_BH = A k_B / (4 ℓ_P²)**

In natural units (k_B = 1): S_BH = A/(4ℓ_P²). ∎

**Remark 14.1 (Citation correspondence with corpus's Hawking-derivation paper).** The Bekenstein-Hawking entropy coefficient η = 1/4 is also derived in [44, Proposition V.1] from the Euclidean Einstein-Hilbert action with the Gibbons-Hawking-York boundary term (York 1972, Gibbons-Hawking 1977) on the Schwarzschild Euclidean cigar. The two derivations agree on η = 1/4; the Step 5 derivation here (via dM = T_H dS) gives the same coefficient through the thermodynamic-integration route. [44, §V] also resolves Hawking's open problem HK-3 (why exactly 1/4): the coefficient is the numerical factor in I_E = βMc²/2 for the Euclidean Schwarzschild geometry, the explicit output of the Gibbons-Hawking-York boundary action with flat-space subtraction K_0.

### 14.2 Hawking radiation as x₄-stationary mode emission from the horizon

The Bekenstein-Hawking area law gives the entropy carried by a horizon as S_BH = k_B A/(4ℓ_P²). We now establish the Hawking 1975 result that **the black hole radiates thermally**, with the physical mechanism identified at the Point level. This resolves Hawking's open problem HK-1 (where does the radiation physically come from?) corpus-internally via [44, Proposition III.1].

**Theorem 14.2 (Hawking radiation as x₄-stationary horizon mode emission, at Point level).** A black hole formed by gravitational collapse emits, at late times after the collapse settles to stationarity, a thermal flux of particles. The radiation is x₄-stationary mode emission from the horizon's pointwise McGucken generator population, thermalized by the Euclidean cigar-geometry periodicity, carried outward by x₄'s expansion at rate c. The flux observed at future null infinity has the Planckian spectrum of blackbody radiation at temperature T_H = ℏκ/(2πck_B) (Theorem 14.3).

The physical mechanism is geometric, not formal: the radiation is **not** a virtual-pair tunneling effect, **not** a vacuum-polarization effect, **not** a barrier-tunneling event [44, Proposition III.1, §III.3].

*Proof — four-step derivation following [44, Proposition III.1].*

**Step 1 (The horizon supports x₄-stationary modes).** The event horizon 𝓗 is a null hypersurface. By Lemma 9.1 (photon x₄-stationarity), null hypersurfaces are exactly the hypersurfaces on which physical excitations are x₄-stationary: their worldlines lie along null geodesics, with |d**x**/dt| = c and dx₄/dt = 0 on the worldline. The horizon is therefore populated by x₄-stationary modes of every quantum field — photons, gravitons, electrons, neutrinos, and so on — with one mode per Planck area ℓ_P² on the horizon by the Point-counting argument of Theorem 14.1, Step 4 [44, Proposition III.1], [8, §11.2].

**Step 2 (These modes are thermalized by the cigar periodicity).** Applying the McGucken Wick rotation τ = x₄/c [14, Theorem 6] to the Lorentzian near-horizon geometry, the Schwarzschild metric becomes the Euclidean cigar with angular period:

> **β_H = 2π/κ = 8πGM/c³**

where κ = c⁴/(4GM) = c²/(2r_s) is the surface gravity. The x₄-stationary horizon modes, previously supported on the Lorentzian horizon null hypersurface, are now supported on the tip of the cigar.

By the standard Kubo-Martin-Schwinger (KMS) condition of quantum statistical mechanics, a quantum field with Euclidean-time periodicity β is thermally distributed at temperature T = ℏ/(k_B β):

> **T_H = ℏ/(k_B β_H) = ℏκ/(2πck_B)**

**Step 3 (Analytic continuation back to Lorentzian produces outgoing thermal radiation).** Analytic continuation of the Euclidean equilibrium distribution back to Lorentzian signature gives a real-time Lorentzian ensemble of horizon modes in thermal equilibrium at T_H. By the same x₄-advance mechanism that carries any x₄-stationary mode outward at rate c (the universal dx₄/dt = ic at every Point of the horizon), these thermal horizon modes propagate outward along null geodesics from the horizon toward future null infinity. The asymptotic observer detects them as a Planckian flux of thermal radiation at temperature T_H.

**Step 4 (The mechanism is geometric, not formal).** Unlike Hawking's original Bogoliubov-coefficient calculation (1975), which is mode-matching between two formal vacuum states ("in" and "out"), the Point-level derivation identifies the physical origin of the radiation: the horizon supports x₄-stationary modes by the null-hypersurface identity (Step 1); these modes are thermalized by the Euclidean cigar periodicity (Step 2); and x₄'s outward expansion at every Point carries them to infinity (Step 3). The radiation is not a virtual-pair tunneling effect; it is the natural emission of the horizon's x₄-stationary mode reservoir, thermalized by the cigar geometry, emitted by the same x₄-expansion mechanism that carries all null signals outward at c. ∎

**Remark 14.2 (Resolution of Hawking's open problem HK-1).** Hawking's 1975 derivation left the physical-origin question open. Three intuitive pictures appear in the literature: virtual-pair production with one member tunneling out, Parikh-Wilczek tunneling, the Unruh-effect generalization. Each captures a different facet without supplying a definitive answer. **The Point-level mechanism in Theorem 14.2 resolves the question**: the radiation is the x₄-stationary mode population of the horizon's pointwise McGucken generators, thermalized by the cigar-geometry periodicity, carried outward by x₄'s expansion. The three intuitive pictures are complementary Lorentzian shadows of this single Euclidean geometric fact:

- **Pair production**: the cigar's symmetric forward-τ / backward-τ branches re-projected to Lorentzian signature
- **Parikh-Wilczek tunneling**: the null-geodesic crossing weighted by the cigar-thermal distribution
- **Unruh-effect generalization**: the near-horizon special case where gravitational redshift makes a static observer effectively accelerated relative to x₄'s expansion

The McGucken dx₄/dt = ic framework supplies the common mechanism that all three pictures shadow [44, §III.3].

### 14.3 The Hawking temperature from the McGucken-Wick cigar period

**Theorem 14.3 (Hawking temperature at Point level).** The temperature of the Hawking radiation at future null infinity is:

> **T_H = ℏκ/(2πck_B)**

where κ is the surface gravity of the horizon. For a Schwarzschild black hole of mass M, κ = c⁴/(4GM), giving T_H ≈ 6.17×10⁻⁸ K · (M_⊙/M). The temperature is the angular period of the McGucken-Wick Euclidean cigar geometry β_H = 2π/κ inverted via the standard KMS thermal-periodicity relation T = ℏ/(k_B β) [44, Proposition IV.1].

*Proof.* By Theorem 14.1 (Step 5), the McGucken Wick rotation τ = x₄/c [14, Theorem 6] applied to the Schwarzschild near-horizon geometry produces a two-dimensional Euclidean cigar with the smooth-tip condition requiring angular period β_H = 2π/κ = 8πGM/c³. By the standard KMS condition, a field on a Euclidean manifold with imaginary-time periodicity β is thermally distributed at temperature T = ℏ/(k_B β) = ℏκ/(2πck_B). ∎

**Remark 14.3 (Resolution of Hawking's open problem HK-2).** Hawking's 1975 paper left open the question of *why* the Euclidean prescription works: in the standard treatment, the Wick rotation is a formal computational device, and its success at reproducing the Hawking temperature has no first-principles justification. **In the McGucken dx₄/dt = ic framework, the Wick rotation is not a formal device but the physical coordinate identification τ = x₄/c** [14, Theorem 6]: removing the i from x₄ = ict gives the real coordinate τ along the fourth axis. The Euclidean cigar period β_H = 2π/κ is therefore not an imaginary-time coincidence but the actual physical period of the x₄-axis at the smooth tip of the cigar at the horizon, and the Hawking temperature is its KMS thermal inversion [44, Proposition IV.1, §IV.3].

### 14.4 Black-hole evaporation from x₄-stationary mode emission

**Theorem 14.4 (Black-hole evaporation at Point level).** The thermal Hawking emission carries energy away from the black hole at rate:

> **dM/dt ∝ −1/M²**

by the Stefan-Boltzmann law applied to the horizon blackbody at temperature T_H = ℏκ/(2πck_B) and area A = 16πG²M²/c⁴. Integrating, a Schwarzschild black hole evaporates completely in time τ ~ (M/M_⊙)³ · 10⁶⁷ yr. The mass-loss law is the Point-level consequence of x₄-stationary mode emission from the horizon at the blackbody rate [44, Proposition VI.1].

*Proof — four steps following [44, Proposition VI.1].*

**Step 1 (Horizon modes are thermal at Planck resolution).** By Theorem 14.2, the x₄-stationary horizon modes form a thermal ensemble at T_H, with mode density one per Planck area ℓ_P² on the horizon (Theorem 14.1, Step 4).

**Step 2 (Stefan-Boltzmann emission).** The energy flux per unit area per unit time from a thermal blackbody at temperature T is σT⁴, where σ = π²k_B⁴/(60ℏ³c²) is the Stefan-Boltzmann constant (a specific combination of c, ℏ, k_B, with c and ℏ both set by the McGucken Principle and substrate-action quantization, Theorem 3.4). The total emission rate from horizon area A at temperature T_H is dE/dt = −σAT_H⁴.

**Step 3 (Mass-loss rate scaling).** For a Schwarzschild black hole: A = 16π(GM/c²)² ∝ M², T_H ∝ 1/M. Therefore:

> dM/dt = −(1/c²)σAT_H⁴ ∝ −M² · (1/M⁴) = **−1/M²**

**Step 4 (Evaporation time).** Integrating dM/dt ∝ −1/M² from initial mass M to 0 gives τ ∝ M³. Plugging in the dimensional prefactor: τ ~ (M/M_⊙)³ · 10⁶⁷ yr. **Primordial black holes of mass ≲ 10¹⁵ g would have fully evaporated by the present epoch** [44, §VI.3]. ∎

**Remark 14.4 (Trans-Planckian dissolution).** The standard Hawking-radiation calculation contains a trans-Planckian problem: the Bogoliubov-coefficient mode-matching extends to arbitrarily high frequencies and requires modes of arbitrarily short wavelength to populate the horizon, well below the Planck scale where the standard QFT-in-curved-spacetime framework cannot be trusted. **In the McGucken dx₄/dt = ic framework this problem dissolves**: by Theorem 3.4, x₄-oscillation is Planck-scale quantized; modes of wavelength shorter than ℓ_P are not independent but represent the same x₄-oscillation state. The trans-Planckian "modes" that appear in the standard Hawking calculation are not physically independent degrees of freedom; they are the same Planck-scale mode viewed in different frequency windows. The physical mode-count on the horizon is bounded at A/ℓ_P² (Theorem 14.1, Step 4); the trans-Planckian regime does not exist as a separate physical domain [44, §VI.4].

### 14.5 Holographic principle

**Theorem 14.5 (Holographic principle at Point level).** The holographic principle of 't Hooft 1993 and Susskind 1995 is the assertion: the bulk McGucken Point distribution in a region V ⊂ 𝓜_G is reconstructed from the family of pointwise operators {ℱ_p}_(p ∈ ∂V) on the boundary, via the Operator-to-Space Theorem [29, Theorem 5.5], [8, Theorem 26], [2, Theorem 24], [2, alias].

*Proof outline.*

- **Step 1**: Boundary ∂V carries pointwise generators {ℱ_p}.
- **Step 2**: Operator-to-Space reconstruction.
- **Step 3 (Kirchhoff–Helmholtz)**: For φ satisfying 𝓓_M φ = 0 in bulk V with boundary data φ|_(∂V):
> φ(y) = ∫_(∂V) [G_+(y,p) n^μ ∂_μ φ(p) − φ(p) n^μ ∂_μ G_+(y,p)] dΣ(p)

with retarded Green's function G_+(y,p) of 𝓓_M.
- **Step 4 (HKLL identification)**:
- Boundary CFT primaries 𝒪(p) ↔ pointwise McGucken generators ℱ_p
- HKLL kernel K_HKLL(y,p) ↔ McGucken retarded Green's function G_+(y,p)
- Kirchhoff–Helmholtz formula ↔ HKLL reconstruction

AdS/CFT is the special case where V is asymptotically AdS and the boundary is conformal infinity. ∎

### 14.6 Information destruction

**Theorem 14.6 (Information destruction).** Information destruction at the macroscopic level is the Geometric-Channel reading of unitary evolution at the Algebraic-Channel level. The two phenomena coexist as readings of the McGucken Point's two d.o.f.

- **Von Neumann entropy** S_vN(ρ̂) = −tr(ρ̂ log ρ̂) is an Algebraic-Channel quantity, conserved under unitary U(t) = exp(−iĤt/ℏ).
- **Thermodynamic entropy** S_thermo(t) = (3k_B/2) ln(4πe·D·t) is a Geometric-Channel quantity, strictly increasing at rate 3k_B/(2t) > 0.

The black-hole information paradox is dissolved at the Point level: the dual-channel architecture makes both readings simultaneously valid. Information is preserved in the von Neumann sense (unitary), and thermodynamic entropy strictly increases (Second Law). The two refer to different aspects of the Point's two d.o.f.

### 14.7 The Refined Generalized Second Law at Point level

The Bekenstein-Hawking area law (§14.1) gives the entropy carried by a black-hole horizon as S_BH = k_B A/(4ℓ_P²). Combined with the matter-entropy Second Law (§12.1), this gives the Generalized Second Law of Bekenstein 1974: the total entropy S_total = S_matter + S_BH never decreases. We restate this at the Point level following [38, Theorem 17].

**Theorem 14.7 (Refined Generalized Second Law at Point level).** For a spacetime partitioned into an exterior region and a horizon-bounded interior, the total entropy S_total satisfies:

> **dS_total/dt = dS_matter/dt + (k_B/4ℓ_P²) · dA/dt ≥ 0**

where A(t) is the horizon area at time t. This is the **global x₄-flux conservation** across the partition: matter entropy accounting for material degrees of freedom outside the horizon, plus the Bekenstein-Hawking area term accounting for the pointwise McGucken generators {ℱ_p}_(p ∈ 𝓗) on the horizon [38, Theorem 17].

*Proof sketch — three steps.*

**Step 1 (Matter-entropy contribution).** For matter Points outside the horizon, dS_matter/dt > 0 strict by Theorem 12.2 (massive-particle ensembles) and Theorem 12.3 (photon ensembles), with rates (3/2)k_B/t and 2k_B/(t−t₀) respectively.

**Step 2 (Horizon-area contribution).** The horizon area A(t) is the sum of pointwise McGucken-generator domains on 𝓗, with each generator ℱ_p occupying one Planck area ℓ_P². When matter falls into the black hole, dA/dt > 0 when Ṁ > 0. The Bekenstein-Hawking area-entropy term contributes (k_B/4ℓ_P²) · dA/dt > 0 to the global entropy budget.

**Step 3 (Global x₄-flux conservation).** The +ic-orientation of the expansive d.o.f. at every Point — matter Points outside the horizon and pointwise generators on the horizon — forces a globally directional x₄-flux. Across the spacetime partition, the total flux into the future is non-decreasing. This is the Point-level source of the inequality: every contribution is non-negative because every ℱ_p has the same +ic-chirality.

The classical Bekenstein 1974 statement assumed S_total as a postulated combination; the McGucken dx₄/dt = ic framework derives the strict monotonicity from the chirality of the McGucken Principle itself [38, Theorem 17].

**Remark 14.5 (Three Second Laws, one chirality).** The McGucken dx₄/dt = ic framework supplies **three** strict-monotonicity Second Laws, all descending from the +ic-chirality of the McGucken Principle:

1. **Massive-particle Second Law** (Theorem 12.2): dS/dt = (3/2)k_B/t > 0 from 3D Brownian spread
2. **Photon Second Law** (Theorem 12.3): dS/dt = 2k_B/(t−t₀) > 0 from 2D spherical-surface spread on Σ⁺(p₀)
3. **Generalized Second Law** (Theorem 14.7): dS_matter/dt + (k_B/4ℓ_P²) · dA/dt ≥ 0 from global x₄-flux conservation across exterior plus horizon-bounded interior

The three Second Laws cover the matter-thermodynamic (1), radiative-thermodynamic (2), and gravitational-thermodynamic (3) sectors. All three close Einstein's third gap (T3 in the Boltzmann-Gibbs program) at the Point level [38, §11, §18].

Phys.* (1995) — Susskind's title; in the McGucken dx₄/dt = ic framework the hologram is the McGucken Sphere Σ⁺(p) generated by dx₄/dt = ic at every Point, and the bulk-to-boundary encoding is the Huygens recursion at atomic resolution (§15)

Phys.* (1998) — AdS/CFT; in the McGucken dx₄/dt = ic framework, AdS/CFT is a special asymptotic case of the universal Huygens-holographic recursion at every Point [45], with the GKP-Witten dictionary derived from x₄-mode counting on the conformal boundary [19, Theorem 23]

> *"I want to know what the show is all about, before it's out."* — **John Archibald Wheeler** [54, Paper 5] — the McGucken Point is the show, dx₄/dt = ic is what it's all about, and the McGucken Sphere is how it manifests at every event

*The principal claim of this section is that the McGucken Point supplies the physical mechanism for holography that the standard literature has explicitly acknowledged it lacks.* For more than three decades — since 't Hooft's 1993 inferential proposal, through Susskind's 1994 extension, Maldacena's 1997 AdS/CFT correspondence (the most-cited paper in theoretical physics history), Bousso's 1999 covariant generalization, Ryu and Takayanagi's 2006 entanglement-entropy realization, Verlinde's 2010 entropic-gravity programme, and the extensive gravity-as-thermodynamics work of Padmanabhan and Jacobson — the holographic principle has been observed, applied, generalized, and exemplified, but never explained. The bulk-to-boundary encoding mechanism that makes the physics of a (d+1)-dimensional bulk encodable on its d-dimensional boundary has remained, in the standard reading, "a deep feature of quantum gravity that has not yet received a explanation." This is the open problem that the present section closes.

The mechanism is direct: ***Huygens' Principle is the holographic principle***, and the McGucken Point is the atomic carrier of both. Every McGucken Point 𝔭 ∈ 𝓜_G is the apex of an outgoing McGucken Sphere Σ⁺(p); the surface of Σ⁺(p) is a set of McGucken Points, each of which is itself the apex of an outgoing Sphere; the iteration is the Huygens construction of how a wavefront at time t generates the wavefront at time t + dt. The bulk content enclosed between the surface at time t and the surface at time t + dt is fully sourced by the Huygens secondary wavelets emanating from the surface-Points at time t. *The surface-Points are the holographic degrees of freedom; the bulk is what they source; the bulk-to-boundary encoding is the Huygens-sourcing relation.* The count of surface-Points (one per Planck-scale cell on the surface area A, from the x₄-mode-counting Theorem 14.1) is the count of independent Huygens sources, which is the count of independent bulk degrees of freedom they generate. This is the Bekenstein bound N_bulk ≤ A/ℓ_P², identified here not as a thermodynamic statement about black holes but as a geometric counting identity holding at every spacetime event.

This is the twelfth containment: **the McGucken Point contains universal holography and AdS/CFT**. The eleven containment theorems of §§4–14 establish that the McGucken Point contains spacetime, gravity, quantum mechanics, symmetry, action, nonlocality, entanglement, the vacuum, entropy's increase and thermodynamics' second law, time and all its arrows and asymmetries, and information at the Point level. The twelfth containment, established in this section, is the Point-level lift of the Universal Geometric Channel Theorem of [1], in which Huygens' Principle is identified with the holographic principle. The content of the section: the standard treatment of the holographic principle has, for more than three decades, lacked a physical mechanism (catalogued below); the McGucken dx₄/dt = ic framework [1] supplies one (the Huygens-Sphere mechanism); the Point-level lift atomically realizes the mechanism at every McGucken Point (Theorem 15.1); four consequences follow including the universal-not-special applicability of holography and the identification of AdS/CFT as a special case (§15.3); and the four-fold collapse of foundational mysteries identifies the Lorentzian-Euclidean equivalence, the holographic principle, gravitational thermodynamics, and AdS/CFT duality as four facets of one geometric process (§15.4).

The standard treatment of the holographic principle has, for more than three decades, lacked a physical mechanism. 't Hooft's 1993 proposal that quantum gravity has a holographic structure reducing the bulk to a boundary was inferred from black-hole entropy considerations without specifying the encoding mechanism. Susskind's 1994 extension added gauge-theoretic and string-theoretic structure but supplied no physical reason why holography should hold. Maldacena's 1997 AdS/CFT correspondence gave a specific concrete duality in anti-de Sitter geometry — a striking and exactly testable example — but the general mechanism remained open. Subsequent work by Ryu and Takayanagi, Bousso, Verlinde, Padmanabhan, and Jacobson generalized, applied, and refined the holographic picture without identifying its source. The standard reading across all of these contributions has been that holography is a deep feature of quantum gravity that has not yet received a explanation.

The McGucken dx₄/dt = ic framework [1] supplies the explanation. The holographic principle is Huygens' Principle. The bulk-to-boundary encoding is the surface-sourcing of bulk wavefronts by Huygens secondary wavelets. The Bekenstein bound is the count of x₄-modes per Planck cell on the McGucken Sphere surface. The universal applicability of holography — not just at black-hole horizons, not just at AdS boundaries, but at every spacetime event — follows because every event is the apex of a McGucken Sphere, and every McGucken Sphere is a holographic screen. The mechanism is dx₄/dt = ic acting at every Point.

Terminological convention: the McGucken-Wick rotation. The signature-bridging substitution τ = x₄/c (equivalently t = −iτ) is used throughout this section and throughout the McGucken corpus [14, 1]. Two distinct interpretations of the same formal substitution are in circulation, and we distinguish them by name. The phrase *Wick rotation* (without modifier) refers to Wick's 1954 formal analytic-continuation device or to standard QFT usage in which t → −iτ is a calculational manoeuvre with no physics of its own. The phrase *McGucken-Wick rotation* refers to the coordinate identification τ = x₄/c on the real four-manifold 𝓜_G whose fourth axis is physically expanding at velocity c via dx₄/dt = ic [14, Theorem 6]. Both phrases name the same formal substitution; the modifier flags which interpretation is operative. In the present paper, every appearance of "Wick rotation" in the context of the McGucken dx₄/dt = ic framework should be read as "McGucken-Wick rotation" — the physical-content reading. The McGucken-Wick rotation's derivation from dx₄/dt = ic is what enables a single physical principle to underlie both Lorentzian and Euclidean derivations across all twelve containments; Wick's 1954 formal device, lacking a physical source, cannot bear this load.

### 15.1 The McGucken Point as atomic generator of universal holography

We state and prove the Point-level lift of [1, Theorem 7.9.5]. The lift is direct: at every Point 𝔭 ∈ 𝓜_G, the recursive Point-Sphere-Point generation structure of the McGucken Point is the atomic-resolution statement of the universal bulk-to-boundary encoding.

**Theorem 15.1 (McGucken Point as atomic generator of universal holography).** *Each McGucken Point 𝔭 = (p, ℱ_p, ψ_p) ∈ 𝓜_G is the apex of a McGucken Sphere Σ⁺(p) of radius R(t) = c(t − t₀) whose surface is a universal holographic screen in the sense of [1, Theorem 7.9.5]. Specifically:*

*(i) The 2-dimensional surface of Σ⁺(p) at radius R has area A(t) = 4π c²(t − t₀)² and carries N_surface = A/ℓ_P² independent x₄-modes, one per Planck-scale cell (Theorem 14.1).*

*(ii) The 3-dimensional bulk enclosed by Σ⁺(p) at time t + dt contains the wavefront propagation in the next interval dt of every Huygens secondary wavelet sourced from the Sphere's surface at time t. Each surface-Point of Σ⁺(p) is itself a McGucken Point and generates its own outgoing Sphere; the new wavefront at time t + dt is the envelope of all these surface-sourced wavelets.*

*(iii) The Huygens-sourcing relation thereby establishes that the bulk content at time t + dt is fully determined by the surface data at time t. This is the holographic encoding of the bulk in the boundary.*

*(iv) The information content of the bulk region is bounded by the surface area of the McGucken Sphere in Planck units: N_bulk(t + dt) ≤ N_surface(t) = A(t)/ℓ_P². This is the Bekenstein bound, identified here as a theorem of dx₄/dt = ic universally — not specifically at black-hole horizons or AdS asymptotic boundaries, but at every spacetime event whose McGucken Sphere serves as a holographic screen.*

*The mechanism is direct: every spacetime Point is the apex of a McGucken Sphere; every McGucken Sphere is a holographic screen for the bulk physics it encloses; the encoding mechanism is Huygens' Principle, which is the surface-sourcing of bulk wavefronts. Holography is not a special feature of black-hole horizons or AdS asymptotic boundaries; it is the universal structure of physics on the McGucken manifold, atomically realized at each McGucken Point.*

**Proof.** The proof is the Point-level lift of the three-step argument of [1, §7.9.4.2].

*Step 1 (Huygens-sourcing as surface-to-bulk map).* From Theorem 12.x (Second Law) and the recursive Point-Sphere-Point structure of the McGucken Point established in §2.4, the McGucken Sphere Σ⁺(p) from Point 𝔭 has radius R(t) = c(t − t₀) and surface area A(t) = 4π c²(t − t₀)². Each Point on the surface of Σ⁺(p) at time t is itself a McGucken Point (the Point Set is closed under Sphere generation; §2.4) and acts as the apex of its own outgoing McGucken Sphere of radius c·dt during the next infinitesimal interval. The new wavefront at time t + dt is the envelope of all these surface-sourced Spheres, which is precisely the McGucken Sphere from 𝔭 at time t + dt. The bulk content at time t + dt is therefore fully determined by the surface-Point data at time t. This is the surface-to-bulk encoding map at the atomic-Point level.

*Step 2 (Surface-to-bulk encoding is the holographic principle).* The standard formulation of the holographic principle ['t Hooft 1993, Susskind 1994] states that the physics of a (d+1)-dimensional bulk region can be fully described by degrees of freedom living on the d-dimensional boundary, with information content bounded by the area of the boundary in Planck units: N_bulk ≤ A_boundary/(4ℓ_P²) = S_BH/k_B. The surface-to-bulk map of Step 1, applied at each Point 𝔭 ∈ 𝓜_G, is exactly this holographic encoding: the boundary is the 2-dimensional Sphere-surface at time t around 𝔭, the bulk is the 3-dimensional region enclosed at time t + dt, and the encoding is the Huygens-sourcing relation between them. The Bekenstein bound is the statement that the number of Huygens sources on the surface (one per Planck cell, by the x₄-mode count of Theorem 14.1) is the maximum number of independent degrees of freedom in the bulk propagation it sources.

*Step 3 (Verification of the count).* From Theorem 14.1, the number of independent x₄-modes on the surface of the McGucken Sphere around 𝔭 at radius R is N_surface = A/ℓ_P² = 4πR²/ℓ_P². Each surface mode is a Huygens source for the bulk propagation in the next interval. The bulk content at the next instant is therefore parametrized by N_surface independent functions (one per Huygens source). The Bekenstein bound N_bulk ≤ N_surface = A/ℓ_P² follows. The McGucken Sphere mode count and the holographic bulk-to-boundary bound are the same count. ∎

*Independent corpus derivation via null-surface-primacy and Planck-cell discretization.* The Point-level Huygens-sourcing derivation above is independently corroborated by the null-surface-primacy derivation of [67, MG-AdSCFT-Formal §2.0 "Mathematical Preliminaries: Formal Definitions"], which reaches the same universal holographic bound S ≤ A/(4ℓ_P²) via a different route: Bekenstein's gravitational-collapse argument applied on null hypersurfaces of the McGucken null foliation, combined with the Planck-cell discretization of the x₄-oscillation. Verbatim from [67, Proposition 2]:

> *"Proposition 2 (Bulk degrees of freedom bounded by boundary). Under A3 and A4, any bulk quantum state in R is encoded in a state of H_Σ on a suitable cross-section Σ of N. Therefore #(bulk states in R) ≤ dim H_Σ = exp(O(A/lₚ²)), so S(R) ≤ (const/lₚ²) A. Proof sketch. From null-surface primacy and A4, the entire radiative content and asymptotic state of R are reconstructible from data on N. From A3, the independent quantum degrees of freedom on Σ ⊂ N are bounded by N = A/lₚ², giving dim H_Σ ~ exp(O(A/lₚ²)). Any attempt to pack more independent degrees of freedom into R leads (by Bekenstein's argument) to gravitational collapse, forming a black hole whose horizon area sets the maximal entropy. Thus the bulk state space is injected into H_Σ."* — [67, Proposition 2]

And the coefficient 1/4 is established at the Hilbert-space-dimension level by [67, Lemma 1] verbatim:

The boundary Hilbert space is H_Σ = ⊗ᵢ Hᵢ, so dim H_Σ = d^N = exp((A/lₚ²) ln d). The maximal entropy is S_max(Σ) = ln dim H_Σ ∝ A/lₚ². With conventional normalization, S_max = A/4lₚ²."* — [67, Lemma 1]

And the unlabeled Area-bound Theorem stated in the body of [67] after Lemma 1, verbatim:

> *"Theorem (Area bound, conditional on A3–A4). Assume M1, null-surface primacy (Definitions 1–3), A3, A4, and standard energy conditions. Then any physically allowed quantum state in a region bounded by null surfaces satisfies S ≤ A/(4lₚ²), where lₚ² is the area quantum associated with the fundamental step of x₄'s expansion."* — [67, Area-bound Theorem]

The two derivations of the universal holographic bound N_bulk ≤ A/ℓ_P² agree on the bound. The Point-level Huygens-sourcing derivation of Steps 1–3 above proceeds through the Point-Sphere-Point recursive structure of §2.4, each surface-Point acting as a Huygens secondary source for the bulk wavefront in the next interval, and the x₄-mode count of Theorem 14.1 supplying one independent Huygens source per Planck cell. The [67] derivation proceeds through the formal null-hypersurface definitions of [67, Definitions 1–3], Assumption A3 (Planck-cell discretization identifying ℓ_P as the fundamental oscillation wavelength of x₄), Assumption A4 (null-surface reconstructibility reducing bulk state count to boundary state count), and Bekenstein's gravitational-collapse argument establishing the horizon area as upper bound on degrees of freedom. The 1/4 coefficient in [67, Lemma 1 and Area-bound Theorem] is established at the Hilbert-space-dimension level via S_max = ln dim H_Σ ∝ A/lₚ² with conventional normalization S_max = A/4lₚ²; the 1/4 coefficient in the McP paper's §15.2 Theorem 15.2 is derived via Lewkowycz–Maldacena replica on the McGucken-Wick n-fold cover.

### 15.2 The McGucken Principle dx₄/dt = ic Reading of Ryu–Takayanagi: Area-Minimization from the Sphere-Chain Bottleneck

**McGucken Principle anchor.** The McGucken Principle dx₄/dt = ic states the physical reality that the fourth dimension is expanding at the velocity of light in a spherically symmetric wavefront from every spacetime event. At every McGucken Point 𝔭 = (p, ℱ_p, ψ_p), the outgoing McGucken Sphere Σ⁺(p) carries the substrate x₄-oscillation, whose substrate wavelength is set by Theorem 3.4 as ℓ_P = √(ℏG/c³). One quantum of action ℏ is delivered per oscillation cycle at the substrate scale (Proposition 3.5). The substrate wavelength ℓ_P is the shortest scale at which the x₄-oscillation supports a stable phase cycle: below ℓ_P the Schwarzschild radius r_S = 2Gm/c² of a substrate quantum of mass m = ℏ/(cλ) exceeds its wavelength, so no shorter-wavelength independent x₄-mode exists. This substrate-wavelength quantization of the McGucken Sphere is the physical origin of the minimum-area-per-mode statement that Theorem 15.2 uses, and it is the statement of [44, §II.5.5, Step 1] and [44, §IX.3, Proposition IX.1] cited verbatim below.

Theorem 15.1 established that the surface of every McGucken Sphere Σ⁺(p) is a universal holographic screen carrying N_surface = A/ℓ_P² independent x₄-modes at Planck-cell density. Theorem 15.2 lifts this to a variational statement: the Ryu–Takayanagi entanglement-entropy formula S(A) = Area(γ_A)/(4G_N) with the minimum-area anchored surface γ_A follows from the McGucken Principle dx₄/dt = ic as a corollary of the substrate-wavelength saturation of x₄-modes on any anchored codim-2 spacelike surface. The area-minimization prescription — which in the standard Ryu–Takayanagi 2006 derivation is imported from the analogy with the black-hole first law — is derived here as the Sphere-chain bottleneck: γ_A is the tightest x₄-stationary anchored surface encoding A's Sphere-chain descendants alone, before mixing with Ā's.

**Theorem 15.2 (Ryu–Takayanagi area-minimization from the Sphere-chain bottleneck).** *Let (M, g) be an asymptotically anti-de Sitter (d+1)-dimensional bulk spacetime with conformal boundary ∂M, and let A ⊂ ∂M be a boundary spatial region with topological boundary ∂A at a fixed boundary time. Under the McGucken Principle dx₄/dt = ic, with the convention z = L²/x₄ identifying the AdS Poincaré radial coordinate z with the McGucken fourth-dimension coordinate x₄ (Lemma 15.2.0 below; the convention is motivated by the Susskind–Witten UV/IR dictionary and the McGucken substrate-wavelength-to-boundary-scale correspondence; §15.3 Consequence 2 below), the entanglement entropy of the boundary reduced density matrix ρ_A = Tr_{Ā}|Ψ⟩⟨Ψ| is given by*

*S(A) = k_B · Area(γ_A) / (4 ℓ_P²) = k_B · Area(γ_A) / (4 G_N)  (natural units ℏ = c = 1),*

*where γ_A is the codim-2 spacelike surface in the bulk anchored on ∂A that minimizes area among all such x₄-stationary spacelike anchored surfaces (equivalently, the x₄-flux-stationary anchored surface in the covariant/HRT case; Lemma 15.2.A below). This is the Ryu–Takayanagi 2006 formula (Ryu–Takayanagi, Phys. Rev. Lett. 96, 181602). The McGucken Principle dx₄/dt = ic derives it as a corollary of three published corpus propositions from [44]: [44, §II.5.5, Step 1] establishes that one x₄-oscillation mode occupies minimum area ℓ_P² on any 2-surface; [44, §IX.3, Proposition IX.1] establishes that every null hypersurface N supports A/ℓ_P² independent x₄-stationary modes and satisfies S ≤ k_B A/(4ℓ_P²) as a theorem, not a postulate; [44, §V.2, Proposition V.1] establishes that the coefficient 1/4 comes from the Gibbons–Hawking–York boundary action I_E = βMc²/2 on the McGucken-Wick cigar via the K − K_0 subtraction. The area-minimization prescription itself is derived here as the Sphere-chain bottleneck (Step 3 below): γ_A is the tightest x₄-stationary anchored surface encoding A's Sphere-chain descendants alone.*

**Proof.** Five steps.

*Step 1 (Substrate wavelength sets minimum mode area on any 2-surface).* The McGucken Principle dx₄/dt = ic expands x₄ spherically symmetrically at velocity c from every spacetime event, with the substrate x₄-oscillation quantized at wavelength ℓ_P (Theorem 3.4). One independent x₄-mode occupies a Planck-scale cell of area ℓ_P² on any 2-dimensional hypersurface. This is the verbatim statement of [44, §II.5.5, Step 1]:

> *"By [MG-Constants], one independent x₄-oscillation mode occupies a minimum area ℓ_P² on any two-dimensional hypersurface. The mode density on a surface of area A is therefore bounded by A/ℓ_P²."* — [44, §II.5.5, Step 1]

Combined with the Compton-clock derivation of Proposition 3.5 above, each x₄-mode carries one action quantum ℏ per oscillation cycle at the substrate wavelength ℓ_P = √(ℏG/c³). The minimum area per x₄-mode on any transverse 2-surface is ℓ_P².

The derivational source of the statement quoted from [44, §II.5.5, Step 1] above is [39, Proposition IV.1] of the companion Bekenstein paper, which gives the substrate-wavelength quantization of the McGucken Sphere in three explicit steps. The wavelength scale is fixed in Step 1 verbatim from [39, §IV, Proposition IV.1, Step 1]:

> *"By [MG-Constants], the fundamental constants c, ℏ, and G are set by x₄'s geometry: c is the rate of x₄-advance (by definition, dx₄/dt = ic), ℏ is the action per Planck-scale increment of x₄-oscillation (established in [MG-Commut] via the derivation of [q, p] = iℏ from dx₄/dt = ic), and G is set by the coupling between x₄'s advance and the curvature of the three-dimensional spatial slice through the Einstein–Hilbert action [MG-SM]. Combining these three by dimensional analysis yields a unique length scale, the Planck length ℓ_P = √(ℏG/c³) ≈ 1.616 × 10⁻³⁵ m. This is the wavelength scale at which x₄'s oscillatory expansion is quantized."* — [39, §IV, Proposition IV.1, Step 1]

The transverse mode-counting is fixed in Step 2 verbatim from [39, §IV, Proposition IV.1, Step 2]:

> *"By the quantization of x₄-oscillation at wavelength ℓ_P (Step 1), two modes separated by less than one Planck length on the horizon are not independent — they represent the same x₄-oscillation state. Independent modes must be separated by at least ℓ_P in both transverse directions on the horizon, so each independent mode occupies an area of at least ℓ_P². The total number of independent modes on a horizon of area A is therefore at most A/ℓ_P². In the limit of maximum entropy — where every Planck area on the horizon is saturated with an independent mode — the count is exactly N_modes = A/ℓ_P². This is the saturation count, and it is the relevant count for a black hole because a black hole is, by the no-hair theorem, the maximum-entropy configuration of given mass, charge, and angular momentum."* — [39, §IV, Proposition IV.1, Step 2]

Together, [39, Proposition IV.1] Steps 1 and 2 derive what [44, §II.5.5, Step 1] states: the minimum-area-per-mode ℓ_P² on any 2-dimensional hypersurface follows from the substrate-wavelength quantization ℓ_P = √(ℏG/c³) of the McGucken Sphere combined with the uncertainty relation applied to the x₄-direction's propagation across the hypersurface, and the count N_modes = A/ℓ_P² saturates in the maximum-entropy configuration. The two citations together give the derivation-plus-statement chain for the minimum-area-per-mode fact used throughout Steps 2–5 below.

*Step 2 (Every anchored codim-2 spacelike x₄-stationary surface carries A/ℓ_P² modes).* Let Σ be a codim-2 spacelike hypersurface in the AdS bulk M with ∂Σ = ∂A ⊂ ∂M, x₄-stationary in the coordinate chart established by Lemma 15.2.0 below (z_AdS and x₄ related by z = L²/x₄; equivalently, all points of Σ share the same x₄-coordinate value, hence the same z-coordinate value under the identification). The McGucken Sphere Σ⁺(p) at each Point p ∈ ∂A generates outgoing x₄-flow into the bulk (Theorem 15.1); the union of these Sphere-chain descendants across ∂A defines a null neighborhood 𝒩(Σ) of Σ in M. By [44, §IX.1, Step 1] the horizon result S_BH = k_B A/(4ℓ_P²) extends to any null hypersurface, and by [44, §IX.3, Proposition IX.1] verbatim:

> *"Let N be any null hypersurface bounding a bulk region R in spacetime. By Step 1 and the Planck-scale quantization of x₄-oscillation [MG-Constants], N supports A/ℓ_P² independent x₄-stationary modes, where A is the area of N. By the six-fold null-surface identity (Step 2), these modes are not independent bits but a correlated dataset whose information content is bounded by the mode count itself. The information content of the bulk region R, measured by the Shannon entropy of its physically accessible quantum states, is therefore bounded by S ≤ k_B A / (4 ℓ_P²). ... The bound holds for every null hypersurface in any spacetime — not only black-hole event horizons — and is therefore the content of the holographic principle as a theorem, not a postulate."* — [44, §IX.3, Proposition IX.1]

The mode density on Σ equals the mode density on 𝒩(Σ) by the substrate-wavelength quantization of Step 1: each x₄-mode occupies one Planck cell of transverse area regardless of whether the anchored surface is null or spacelike. Every x₄-stationary anchored codim-2 spacelike surface Σ with ∂Σ = ∂A therefore carries N(Σ) = Area(Σ)/ℓ_P² independent x₄-modes.

*Formal null-hypersurface grounding from [67, MG-AdSCFT-Formal §2.0].* The null-hypersurface framework used in this step and its curved-spacetime extension to the AdS bulk are grounded in the six formal definitions of [67, §2.0 "Mathematical Preliminaries: Formal Definitions"]. Verbatim from [67, Definition 1]: *"Let (M, gμν) be a 4-dimensional Lorentzian manifold. A C² hypersurface N ⊂ M is null if its normal covector field nμ satisfies gμν nμ nν = 0 at every point of N. Equivalently, the pullback of the metric to TN is degenerate."* This is the formal null-hypersurface definition invoked (informally) at the head of [44, §IX.3, Proposition IX.1] quoted above. Verbatim from [67, Definition 2]: *"In flat Minkowski space (ℝ¹·³, η) with metric η = −c²dt² + dx², define Φ(xμ) = |x| − ct. For each τ ≥ 0, the level set N_τ := {xμ ∈ M : Φ(xμ) = 0, t = τ} is a 2-sphere of radius cτ sitting in the null cone |x|² − c²t² = 0. The forward light cone C⁺ = {Φ = 0, t > 0} is a null hypersurface, and each N_τ is a spacelike codimension-2 cross-section of C⁺. The family {N_τ}_{τ≥0} is the McGucken null foliation."* The McGucken null foliation identified here is exactly the outgoing McGucken Sphere Σ⁺(p) foliation used throughout Theorem 15.1 and Theorem 15.2 Step 2 above. The codim-2 spacelike anchored surfaces Σ ∈ 𝒜(∂A) of the present step are curved-spacetime generalizations of the McGucken null foliation cross-sections N_τ of [67, Definition 2]. Verbatim from [67, Definition 4] for the curved-spacetime extension: *"A family {N_λ}_{λ∈I} of null hypersurfaces is a null foliation if: (i) the N_λ are disjoint except on their boundaries; (ii) ∪_{λ∈I} N_λ covers an open region of spacetime; (iii) each N_λ is generated by null geodesics."* This is the formal statement of the Sphere-chain descendants of ∂A into the AdS bulk described above. And verbatim from [67, Definition 5]: *"Under a conformal rescaling gμν → Ω²gμν with Ω > 0, the null condition gμν nμ nν = 0 is preserved. Null hypersurfaces are conformally invariant structures — the geometric basis for the conformal symmetry of boundary theories in AdS/CFT."* The conformal invariance of null hypersurfaces is a standard feature of AdS/CFT boundary conformal symmetry; the specific coordinate convention z = L²/x₄ used in Lemma 15.2.0 below is a separate choice (motivated as described there). [67, Assumption A3] (Planck-cell discretization identifying ℓ_P with the fundamental oscillation wavelength of x₄) parallels the substrate-wavelength quantization of Step 1 above, with both using ℓ_P as the elementary cell scale.

The derivational grounding of the lift to arbitrary null hypersurfaces used in [44, §IX.1, Step 1] rests on [39, Proposition III.1] of the companion Bekenstein paper, which establishes the horizon → null hypersurface → x₄-stationary support chain in four steps. The relevant identification is Step 2 of that Proposition. Verbatim from [39, §III, Proposition III.1, Step 2]:

> *"By Proposition IV.1 of [MG-Twistor], a physical excitation has a null four-momentum if and only if it is x₄-stationary. The McGucken Proof [MG-Proof, F1] establishes this as a theorem of the invariant four-speed condition: an object moving at |v| = c through the three spatial dimensions has exhausted its four-speed budget on spatial motion and has zero advance in x₄. Null hypersurfaces are therefore the hypersurfaces on which x₄-stationary physical excitations are supported. The horizon, being null, is precisely such a hypersurface."* — [39, §III, Proposition III.1, Step 2]

*Disambiguation of "[MG-Twistor]" as cited in the [39] quote above.* In [39, MG-Bekenstein]'s own bibliography, "[MG-Twistor]" refers to the April 20, 2026 companion paper *"How the McGucken Principle of a Fourth Expanding Dimension Gives Rise to Twistor Space: dx₄/dt = ic as the Physical Mechanism Underlying Penrose's Twistor Theory"* — entry [65, MG-TwistorRise] in the present bibliography. The specific Proposition IV.1 cited in the [39] verbatim quote above is [65, §IV, Proposition IV.1], whose statement and proof are verbatim from [65]:

> *"Proposition IV.1 (Null = x₄-stationary). A worldline in Minkowski spacetime is null (lightlike) if and only if its tangent has zero x₄-advance: |dx₄/dt| = 0. Equivalently, photons are stationary in x₄. Proof. A worldline γ is null iff ds² = 0 along γ. In coordinates with x₄ = ict, ds² = |dx→|² − c²dt² = |dx→|² + dx₄², so ds² = 0 requires |dx→|² = −dx₄² = c²dt² (using dx₄ = ic dt). Taking the magnitude along γ, |dx→/dt|² = c² = |v|². By Proposition II.2, |dx₄/dt|² = c² − |v|² = 0. Thus null worldlines have zero x₄-advance. Conversely, |dx₄/dt| = 0 implies |v|² = c² by Proposition II.2, giving ds² = c²dt² − c²dt² = 0, i.e., null. ∎"* — [65, §IV, Proposition IV.1]

The invariant four-speed formulation invoked in the [39] quote as *"an object moving at |v| = c through the three spatial dimensions has exhausted its four-speed budget on spatial motion and has zero advance in x₄"* is [65, Proposition II.2] — the McGucken master equation |dx→/dt|² + |dx₄/dt|² = c² partitioning the universal rate c between spatial motion and x₄-advance, from which Proposition IV.1 follows in two lines as reproduced above.

*Disambiguation of "[MG-Proof, F1]" as cited in the [39] quote above.* The [66, MG-Proof] paper *"The McGucken Principle and Proof: The Fourth Dimension Is Expanding at the Velocity of Light dx₄/dt = ic as a Foundational Law of Physics"* (April 15, 2026) has twelve named theorems labeled A through I plus Lemma M.1, Theorem D′, and Theorem G.1; there is no formal *"Fact F1"* label in [66]. The [39] *"F1"* citation appears to be an informal shorthand for the physical fact ("Fact 1" of the McGucken Proof) that the invariant four-speed of every worldline is exactly c, spent variably between spatial motion and x₄-advance. The composite statement [39] attributes to *"[MG-Proof, F1]"* is stated formally in [66] across three related places, all reproduced verbatim from [66]:

> *"Theorem C (Master Equation — Invariant Four-Speed). Define the four-velocity u^µ := dx^µ/dτ, µ ∈ {1, 2, 3, 4}. Then, for every timelike worldline, u^µ u_µ = −c². The invariant magnitude of the four-velocity is a theorem of Axiom M, not an independent kinematical principle."* — [66, Theorem C]

> *"Corollary C.2 (Four-velocity budget constraint). The three spatial components and the fourth component of the four-velocity satisfy the 'budget' identity |v|²γ² + (u^4)² = −c², with (u^4)² = (icγ)² = −c²γ² in the Euclidean chart. As |v| → 0, all of the four-speed is carried by u^4 (the x₄-direction); as |v| → c, γ → ∞ and the spatial four-velocity components dominate while the x₄ component remains imaginary of magnitude cγ."* — [66, Corollary C.2]

> *"Theorem F Case (2) (Lightlike motion, photon at absolute rest in x₄). In the McGucken framework, the photon is at absolute rest in x₄ in the sense of riding the x₄-wavefront: it surfs the expansion of x₄ rather than independently advancing through it."* — [66, Theorem F Case (2)]

[66, Theorem C], [66, Corollary C.2], and [66, Theorem F Case (2)] stand verbatim above as the McGucken Proof's statement of the invariant-four-speed material cited in the [39] quote as "[MG-Proof, F1]". The relation to [65, Proposition II.2] above is through the shared master-equation structure: [65] states |d x /dt|² + |dx₄/dt|² = c² in coordinate-time parameterization; [66, Theorem C] states u^µ u_µ = −c² in proper-time parameterization. Both are derived from Axiom M (dx₄/dt = ic).

The lift from horizon to arbitrary null hypersurface used in [44, §IX.1, Step 1] rests on this identification: null hypersurfaces in any spacetime support x₄-stationary physical excitations at Planck-cell density, not only black-hole event horizons. The Sphere-chain descendants of ∂A into the bulk fill the null neighborhood 𝒩(Σ) with x₄-stationary modes at density 1/ℓ_P² of transverse area (by the outgoing McGucken Sphere generated at c from each Point of ∂A; Theorem 15.1), and the anchored codim-2 spacelike Σ inherits the same density by the substrate-wavelength quantization of Step 1 above.

*Step 3 (Sphere-chain bottleneck: γ_A as min-cut of the past Sphere-chain flow from A into the bulk).* The past Sphere-chain descendants of A ⊂ ∂M into the bulk M form a definite flow, generated at c by the outgoing McGucken Sphere Σ⁺(p) at every boundary Point p ∈ A. By the Reciprocal Generation Property of the McGucken Sphere ([29, Theorem 5.6]; §2.4 above), this flow is divergence-free on the bulk: the count of independent Sphere-chain trajectories from A across any codim-1 bulk slice is conserved along the flow direction.

Let 𝒜(∂A) denote the set of all codim-2 spacelike x₄-stationary anchored surfaces Σ ⊂ M with ∂Σ = ∂A, homologous to A on ∂M (in the sense that Σ ∪ A bounds a codim-1 spacelike region in the bulk). By Step 2 above, every Σ ∈ 𝒜(∂A) carries N(Σ) = Area(Σ)/ℓ_P² independent x₄-modes at Planck-cell density. Classify each mode m on Σ by tracing its past Sphere-chain trajectory back to ∂M via the incoming x₄-flow: the trajectory terminates on a subset s(m) ⊂ ∂M consisting of the boundary points from which the mode's past Sphere-chain propagates. In the static case s(m) is a single point (unique past Sphere-chain trajectory in the AdS Poincaré or global chart); in the covariant/HRT case s(m) is the bounded null-generator projection of the mode's past bulk trajectory onto ∂M. The classification into sectors is:

(i) s(m) ⊂ A: the mode's past Sphere-chain trajectory originates entirely within A.

(ii) s(m) ⊂ Ā: the mode's past Sphere-chain trajectory originates entirely within Ā = ∂M ∖ A.

(iii) s(m) ∩ A ≠ ∅ and s(m) ∩ Ā ≠ ∅: the mode's past Sphere-chain trajectory has boundary termination points in both A and Ā, so the mode carries A + Ā correlation.

This classification is definitional and depends only on the Sphere-chain flow structure from ∂M into the bulk. No candidate bulk surface γ_A enters the definition; the sectors are determined entirely by the outgoing McGucken Sphere Σ⁺(p) generated at each boundary Point p and by the resulting bulk flow.

Only sector-(i) modes contribute to the diagonal Shannon count S(A) = −Tr(ρ_A log ρ_A) for the reduced density matrix ρ_A = Tr_{Ā}|Ψ⟩⟨Ψ|. Sector-(ii) modes are traced out; sector-(iii) modes contribute to off-diagonal correlator terms of ρ_A but not to the diagonal probability weights that define the Shannon entropy of ρ_A.

The count N_(i)(Σ) of sector-(i) modes on any Σ ∈ 𝒜(∂A) equals the count of independent past Sphere-chain trajectories from A that cross Σ. By the Sphere-chain conservation established above (Reciprocal Generation, [29, Theorem 5.6]), this count is invariant under homological deformation of Σ within 𝒜(∂A): every anchored surface homologous to A carries the same number of A-originating trajectory crossings. Denote this invariant count N_flow(A). The substrate-wavelength saturation of Step 2 gives the upper bound:

N_flow(A) ≤ Area(Σ)/ℓ_P²   for every Σ ∈ 𝒜(∂A),

because Σ has at most Area(Σ)/ℓ_P² independent modes total, of which the sector-(i) subset is N_flow(A).

γ_A's dimensional structure that makes the max-flow / min-cut framework applicable. In the (d+1)-dim asymptotically AdS bulk with the d-dim boundary CFT on ∂M and A ⊂ ∂M a spatial region at fixed boundary time, γ_A is a codim-2 surface in the bulk (dimension d − 1). Restricted to the d-dim bulk spatial slice at fixed t (well-defined in the static case; replaced by an achronal maximin slice in the covariant/HRT case per Wall 2014, Class. Quant. Grav. 31 (2014) 225007, arXiv:1211.3494), γ_A becomes a codim-1 hypersurface within the slice (dimension d − 1 embedded in a d-dim slice) — a proper hypersurface separating the slice into two connected regions, one anchored on A ⊂ ∂M and the other anchored on Ā ⊂ ∂M. This codim-1-in-slice status of γ_A is what enables the max-flow / min-cut framework: a "cut" in the graph-theoretic and the geometric-measure-theoretic sense is a codim-1 hypersurface whose removal disconnects source from sink at the slice level, and γ_A is precisely such a hypersurface for the Sphere-chain flow from A into the bulk spatial slice.

The max-flow / min-cut inequality for the Sphere-chain flow on the Riemannian bulk spatial slice (Federer, *Geometric Measure Theory*, Springer 1969, Grundlehren 153, Ch. 5, for the geometric-measure-theoretic max-flow / min-cut theorem via area-minimizing integral currents; Freedman–Headrick, Commun. Math. Phys. 352 (2017) 407–438, arXiv:1604.00354, for the specific RT-setting max-flow / min-cut duality via divergence-free bit threads which is the subject of Corollary 15.2.B below) gives:

N_flow(A) ≤ min_{Σ ∈ 𝒜(∂A)} Area(Σ) / ℓ_P².

Define γ_A as the minimizer:

γ_A := argmin_{Σ ∈ 𝒜(∂A)} Area(Σ).

Existence in the static asymptotically AdS bulk follows from the classical existence theorem for area-minimizing integral currents in a fixed homology class (Federer 1969, Ch. 5). The homology constraint on the RT surface is the requirement that Σ ∪ A bound a codim-1 spacelike region in the bulk spatial slice, established for the RT setting by Headrick–Takayanagi (Phys. Rev. D 76 (2007) 106013, arXiv:0704.3719) and reviewed together with the RT existence structure by Headrick (JHEP 03 (2014) 085, arXiv:1312.6717). Uniqueness of the minimizer holds generically but can fail at holographic entanglement phase transitions, where two candidate minimizers of the same area exchange dominance as the boundary region A is deformed (Headrick 2014, ibid., §3); at such phase transitions γ_A is defined as the area-minimizing candidate satisfying the homology constraint, with the transition point signaled by a discontinuity in the derivative of S(A) with respect to the region parameters. In the covariant/HRT case γ_A is the extremal (saddle-point) surface of the area functional subject to the anchor and homology constraints, established by the maximin construction of Wall 2014 (arXiv:1211.3494) and the replica-trick derivation of Step 4 below (Dong–Lewkowycz–Rangamani 2016, JHEP 11 (2016) 028, arXiv:1607.07506). The Sphere-chain minimizer of the present Step 3 coincides with the RT/HRT extremal surface by Lemma 15.2.A below.

The max-flow / min-cut duality of Federer 1969 (applied to the Sphere-chain flow) gives equality at γ_A:

N_flow(A) = Area(γ_A) / ℓ_P².

Every past Sphere-chain trajectory from A crosses γ_A exactly once (min-cut saturation, standard geometric-measure-theoretic max-flow / min-cut), and by the flow classification above every crossing mode on γ_A has s(m) ⊂ A: it is a sector-(i) mode. Therefore γ_A carries exactly Area(γ_A)/ℓ_P² sector-(i) modes with no sector-(ii) modes and no sector-(iii) modes — γ_A saturates the substrate-wavelength Planck-cell bound with all modes in sector (i).

For any Σ ∈ 𝒜(∂A) with Area(Σ) > Area(γ_A), the sector-(i) count on Σ remains N_(i)(Σ) = N_flow(A) = Area(γ_A)/ℓ_P² (Sphere-chain conservation); the excess (Area(Σ) − Area(γ_A))/ℓ_P² modes above the sector-(i) count are sector-(ii) trajectories from Ā that cross Σ, or sector-(iii) correlator modes. These excess modes are traced out (sector-(ii)) or contribute only to off-diagonal correlators (sector-(iii)) in ρ_A, and therefore do not increase the diagonal Shannon count.

Combining, the entanglement entropy of ρ_A is fixed by the sector-(i) mode count on γ_A with the coefficient η fixed in Step 4:

S(A) = −Tr(ρ_A log ρ_A) = k_B · N_flow(A) · η = k_B · Area(γ_A) / ℓ_P² · η.

The characterization of γ_A as the min-cut of the Sphere-chain flow from A is non-circular: γ_A is defined by the extremum of the area functional on 𝒜(∂A), and its role as the sector-(i) / sector-(ii) separator falls out of the Federer max-flow / min-cut theorem applied to the Sphere-chain flow. The area-minimization prescription of the standard Ryu–Takayanagi 2006 formulation is thereby derived — rather than imported — as the Sphere-chain bottleneck condition on the McGucken Sphere flow from A into the bulk. The independent derivation via the Lewkowycz–Maldacena replica cover of Step 4 below reproduces the same γ_A as the n → 1 limit of the branch locus, confirming the coincidence of the min-cut characterization (Step 3) with the extremality characterization (Step 4).

*Step 4 (The coefficient η = 1/4 from the Lewkowycz–Maldacena replica computation on the McGucken-Wick n-fold cover).* The Signature-Bridging Theorem 5.5 above establishes that Lorentzian and Euclidean readings of the same substrate physics agree under the McGucken-Wick rotation t → −iτ with τ = x₄/c. Applied to the AdS bulk, this produces the Euclidean AdS bulk on which the entanglement entropy S(A) is computed by the replica-trick partition-function method of Lewkowycz–Maldacena (JHEP 08 (2013) 090; arXiv:1304.4926), with the covariant/HRT extension established by Dong–Lewkowycz–Rangamani (JHEP 11 (2016) 028; arXiv:1607.07506).

The replica computation. The Rényi entropy of order n is

S_n(A) = (1/(1−n)) log Tr(ρ_A^n) = (1/(1−n)) [log Z_n − n log Z_1],

where Z_n is the Euclidean partition function of the boundary CFT on the n-fold branched cover of the base boundary manifold, with branch locus ∂A. By the AdS/CFT correspondence (Maldacena 1998, Adv. Theor. Math. Phys. 2 (1998) 231, arXiv:hep-th/9711200; Gubser–Klebanov–Polyakov 1998, Phys. Lett. B 428 (1998) 105, arXiv:hep-th/9802109; Witten 1998, Adv. Theor. Math. Phys. 2 (1998) 253, arXiv:hep-th/9802150; the McGucken Principle dx₄/dt = ic reading is [MG-AdSCFT §4.1 "Bulk wave equations and boundary operator data", §5.2 "The chain from dx₄/dt = ic to AdS/CFT"] and §15.3 Consequence 2 below), Z_n equals the on-shell bulk gravitational partition function of the McGucken-Wick Euclidean bulk with boundary condition set by the n-fold branched cover on ∂M:

Z_n = ∫ 𝒟g · e^(−I_E[g]) |_{∂M = n-fold branched cover with branch locus ∂A}.

In the semiclassical (large-N, large-central-charge) limit, this integral is dominated by a bulk saddle-point geometry g_n: an n-fold branched cover of the Euclidean AdS bulk with a codim-2 branch locus γ_A^(n) in the interior, whose position is fixed by the bulk Einstein equations on the covered geometry.

Branch-locus geometry. The standard LM construction has local Euclidean geometry near γ_A^(n) that is a cone of opening angle 2π/n at each transverse cross-section, with the n = 1 case recovering a smooth cover with no conical defect. The horizon case of [44, §II.3, Step 2] is the n = 1 instance of this construction restricted to a Killing horizon, with the Euclidean-time period β = 2π/κ fixed by the smooth-closure condition on the Euclidean cigar. The general LM branched cover extends the smooth-cigar construction of [44, §II.3] from n = 1 to arbitrary integer n ≥ 1.

Extremality of γ_A from the replica saddle. The Einstein equations for g_n imply, in the n → 1 limit, that the branch locus γ_A^(1) := γ_A satisfies the codim-2 extremality condition δArea/δX^μ = 0 for variations δX^μ of the surface embedding subject to δX^μ|_{∂A} = 0. This is the central result of Lewkowycz–Maldacena in the static case and of Dong–Lewkowycz–Rangamani in the covariant/HRT case. In the McGucken Principle dx₄/dt = ic reading (Lemma 15.2.A below), the extremality condition is the x₄-flux-stationarity of γ_A: δΦ_x₄/δX^μ = (1/ℓ_P²) δArea/δX^μ = 0 by the mode-density identity of Step 2 above. The replica derivation therefore reproduces the same γ_A as the Sphere-chain-flow min-cut of Step 3 above, from an independent starting point: Step 3 characterizes γ_A as the min-cut of the outgoing McGucken Sphere flow from A; Step 4 characterizes γ_A as the n → 1 limit of the replica-cover branch locus. The two characterizations are consistent (Lemma 15.2.A below establishes the equivalence for the static and covariant cases).

Evaluation of the on-shell action on the n-fold cover. Lewkowycz–Maldacena establish that the on-shell Euclidean action of g_n, evaluated to first order in (n − 1), is:

I_E[g_n] = n · I_E[g_1] + (n − 1) · A(γ_A) / (4G_N) + O((n − 1)²).

The formula is defined up to an n-independent additive constant absorbed into I_E[g_1], and the coefficient of the (n − 1)¹ linear term is uniquely fixed by the physical boundary condition at n = 1: the branch locus degenerates to a smooth codim-2 surface with no conical defect at n = 1, forcing the coefficient of the (n − 1)⁰ term in the "conical-defect contribution" part to vanish, so the (n − 1)¹ coefficient is the leading physical contribution. This is the same non-ambiguity that fixes the coefficient 1/2 in I_E = βMc²/2 for the Schwarzschild computation of [44, §V.2]: the additive constant vanishes in the appropriate limit (K − K_0 subtraction removing the flat-space contribution in the Schwarzschild case; n = 1 smooth-cover degeneracy in the general replica case), and the leading physical coefficient is uniquely determined.

The first term in the expansion above is the trivial n-fold copy of the base on-shell action (which is n · I_E[g_1] because the smooth n-fold cover away from γ_A contributes n times the base action per copy). The second term is the codim-2 conical-defect contribution from the branch locus γ_A, computed by regularizing the conical singularity with a smooth cap of small transverse radius ε and integrating the Einstein–Hilbert Lagrangian on the regulated cap; the transverse Gauss–Bonnet integral on the 2-cap gives the concentrated Ricci contribution at strength 4π(1 − 1/n) = 4π(n − 1)/n + O((n − 1)²), and integrating along γ_A with the Einstein–Hilbert normalization prefactor 1/(16πG_N) and the sign appropriate to the Euclidean gravity convention gives (n − 1) · A(γ_A) / (4G_N) at leading order. The positive sign is fixed by the physical constraint that I_E[g_n] − n · I_E[g_1] be non-negative for n > 1 (dual to log Tr(ρ_A^n) ≤ 0 for a density matrix with eigenvalues bounded above by unity). The specific evaluation is Lewkowycz–Maldacena 2013 (JHEP 08:090); reproduced with different regulator schemes in the subsequent literature (Fursaev–Patrushev–Solodukhin, Phys. Rev. D 88 (2013) 044054, arXiv:1306.4000, for the direct cone regularization via the "squashed cone" distributional geometry; Dong 2014, JHEP 01:044, arXiv:1310.5713, for the extension to higher-derivative gravity via the squashed-cone method).

The entanglement entropy from the replica limit. Substituting into the Rényi definition S_n(A) = (1/(1 − n)) · log Tr(ρ_A^n) = (1/(1 − n)) · [log Z_n − n · log Z_1] = (1/(1 − n)) · [−I_E[g_n] + n · I_E[g_1]]:

S_n(A) = (1/(1 − n)) · [−(n − 1) · A(γ_A) / (4G_N) + O((n − 1)²)] = A(γ_A) / (4 G_N) + O(n − 1).

The result is exact at leading order in (n − 1): the (1 − n) factor in the denominator cancels the −(n − 1) numerator, giving S(A) = lim_{n → 1} S_n(A) = A(γ_A) / (4 G_N) directly, without needing L'Hôpital's rule. (The consistency check: S(A) = −∂_n Tr(ρ_A^n)|_{n=1} = log Z_1 − ∂_n log Z_n|_{n=1} = −I_E[g_1] + ∂_n I_E[g_n]|_{n=1} = −I_E[g_1] + [I_E[g_1] + A(γ_A)/(4G_N)] = A(γ_A)/(4G_N), matching.)

Restoring k_B (dropped by the convention of setting ℏ = c = 1 above), and using the McGucken-Constants identity ℓ_P² = ℏG_N/c³ [MG-Constants; see [39, Proposition IV.1, Step 1] verbatim in Step 1 above]:

S(A) = k_B · A(γ_A) / (4 ℓ_P²).

Therefore η = 1/4.

Relation to the Schwarzschild [44, §V.2] computation. The Schwarzschild derivation of η = 1/4 given in [44, §V.2, Proposition V.1] is the special case of the Lewkowycz–Maldacena replica computation above in which γ_A is a Killing horizon and the n-fold cover reduces to a periodic Euclidean-time identification with period β = 2π/κ along the Killing direction. Verbatim from [44, §V.2, Proposition V.1]:

> *"The McGucken Wick rotation of the Schwarzschild geometry produces a Euclidean manifold that is smooth everywhere — the 'cigar' near the horizon, extending to an asymptotically flat region at spatial infinity. The total Euclidean gravitational action, evaluated on this manifold with the Gibbons–Hawking–York boundary term included, is I_E = β M c²/2, where β = 2π/κ is the Euclidean-time period forced by smoothness at the horizon (§II.3) and M is the ADM mass. The Euclidean partition-function relation S = (β⟨E⟩ − I_E) k_B/ℏ, with ⟨E⟩ = Mc², yields S_BH = k_B A/(4ℓ_P²). The factor 1/4 is exactly the numerical factor appearing in I_E = βMc²/2, and it has a direct geometric origin: the Euclidean action of the Schwarzschild cigar is half of the on-shell energy integral because the horizon contributes a single Gibbons–Hawking–York boundary term whose coefficient is 1/2 in the standard normalization."* — [44, §V.2, Proposition V.1]

For a Killing horizon, the codim-2 conical-defect contribution at the branch locus computed in the Lewkowycz–Maldacena replica evaluation above equals the horizon Gibbons–Hawking–York boundary term of [44, §V.2, Proposition V.1] because the horizon's Killing symmetry trivializes the branched cover to the periodic ℤ_n identification along the Killing direction; the two evaluations coincide and both give η = 1/4. The Killing-horizon-specific I_E = βMc²/2 relation is the special-case output of the general Lewkowycz–Maldacena action evaluation restricted to Killing branches.

For a general RT surface γ_A that is not a Killing horizon (as is the case for the RT surface of a generic boundary region A in AdS/CFT), the Killing-symmetry-based partition-function relation of [44, §V.2, Step 4] does not directly apply, and the Lewkowycz–Maldacena replica computation above is the correct derivation. The physical meaning of the 1/4 in the McGucken dx₄/dt = ic framework is stated verbatim in [44, §V.2, Meaning] for the Schwarzschild case:

> *"The 1/4 in the Bekenstein–Hawking formula is the numerical factor in I_E = βMc²/2 for the Euclidean Schwarzschild geometry. This factor is neither a Bogoliubov-coefficient output nor a heuristic; it is the explicit computation of the Gibbons–Hawking–York boundary action on the Euclidean Schwarzschild cigar, with the subtraction term K_0 (which removes the vacuum-flat-space contribution) generating the 1/2. In the McGucken framework, this is a real geometric fact about the Euclidean manifold obtained by removing the i from x₄ at the horizon, not a formal trick."* — [44, §V.2, Meaning]

The extension to the RT case: the 1/4 in Theorem 15.2 is a real geometric fact about the McGucken-Wick Euclidean manifold obtained by removing the i from x₄ at the branch locus γ_A of the Lewkowycz–Maldacena n-fold cover. The two geometric interpretations of the 1/4 coincide on Killing horizons (where the branch locus is the horizon) and differ in evaluation method for general γ_A (where the branch locus is not a Killing horizon, and the Lewkowycz–Maldacena conical-defect evaluation replaces the [44, §V.2] GHY horizon-boundary evaluation). Both are theorems of the McGucken Principle dx₄/dt = ic via the McGucken-Wick rotation of the underlying Lorentzian geometry (Theorem 5.5 above).

*Step 5 (Combining Steps 1–4).* Combining Steps 1 through 4, the entanglement entropy of the boundary region A ⊂ ∂M in an asymptotically AdS bulk under the McGucken Principle dx₄/dt = ic is

S(A) = k_B · Area(γ_A) / (4 ℓ_P²),

where γ_A is the codim-2 spacelike anchored surface with minimum area in the x₄-stationary family 𝒜(∂A) — equivalently, the tightest Sphere-chain bottleneck of A's descendant modes before mixing with Ā's. This is the Ryu–Takayanagi 2006 formula (Ryu–Takayanagi, Phys. Rev. Lett. 96, 181602), derived here as a corollary of the McGucken Principle dx₄/dt = ic via the three published corpus propositions [44, §II.5.5, Step 1], [44, §IX.3, Proposition IX.1], and [44, §V.2, Proposition V.1] combined with the Sphere-chain bottleneck argument of Step 3 above, the Lewkowycz–Maldacena replica argument of Step 4 above, and the coordinate identification of Lemma 15.2.0 below. The published corpus statement of the mode-count form of the same theorem is [19, Theorem 35] verbatim: *"RT formula as x₄-stationary mode count: S(A) = Area(γ̃)/(4ℓ_P²) k_B"*. The narrative reading of the RT formula in the McGucken dx₄/dt = ic framework is [MG-AdSCFT §4.3 "Entanglement entropy and extremal surfaces"] verbatim: *"the entanglement entropy is related to the number of independent null-surface channels (in Planck-area units) that thread through the minimal bulk surface connecting the boundary region to its complement. This interpretation is heuristic rather than derived — the standard derivation uses the replica trick in Euclidean quantum gravity — but it provides a physical picture that complements existing 'bit thread' and tensor-network interpretations of holographic entanglement."* The present Theorem 15.2 supplies the rigorous derivation that [MG-AdSCFT §4.3] flags as needed: Step 4's Lewkowycz–Maldacena replica computation on the McGucken-Wick n-fold cover IS the replica-trick derivation, and Step 3's Sphere-chain bottleneck IS the physical mechanism supplying the area-minimization prescription. The present derivation therefore closes the gap between [19, Theorem 35] as a mode-count statement and the Ryu–Takayanagi formula as a variational statement, and closes the "heuristic rather than derived" gap [MG-AdSCFT §4.3] itself identifies. ∎

**Lemma 15.2.0 (Identification of the AdS Poincaré radial coordinate z with the McGucken fourth-dimension coordinate x₄).**

*McGucken Principle anchor.* The McGucken Principle dx₄/dt = ic states the physical reality that the fourth dimension is expanding at the velocity of light c in a spherically symmetric wavefront from every spacetime event. In the asymptotically anti-de Sitter (d+1)-dimensional bulk (M, g) of Maldacena 1998 (Adv. Theor. Math. Phys. 2 (1998) 231, arXiv:hep-th/9711200) in Poincaré coordinates (t, z, x_i) with metric ds² = (L²/z²)(−dt² + dz² + Σ_i dx_i²), the bulk radial coordinate z ∈ (0, ∞) has conformal boundary at z → 0 and Poincaré horizon at z → ∞. In the standard AdS/CFT framework z is a formal coordinate with no assigned physical mechanism; the McGucken Principle dx₄/dt = ic supplies the mechanism.

*Statement.* We adopt the identification between the AdS Poincaré radial coordinate z and the McGucken fourth-dimension coordinate x₄

z = L² / x₄,     x₄ > 0,     L > 0,

where L is the AdS radius. The mapping is a monotone-decreasing bijection between (0, ∞) in x₄ and (0, ∞) in z: the conformal boundary z → 0 corresponds to x₄ → ∞, and the Poincaré horizon z → ∞ corresponds to x₄ → 0⁺. The restriction x₄ > 0 is fixed by the McGucken Principle's dx₄/dt = +ic irreversibility (x₄'s advance has a definite sign), established independently in two companion papers of the same April 20, 2026 date: [65, Proposition VII.1: chirality from x₄-irreversibility] and [68, Proposition VII.1: chirality from dx₄/dt = +ic]. Verbatim from [68, Proposition VII.1]:

> *"The chirality of twistor space is the geometric statement that dx₄/dt = +ic is the physical direction of x₄'s advance, not −ic. Self-dual fields correspond to configurations aligned with this arrow; anti-self-dual fields correspond to the conjugate direction. Because the universe has a definite arrow of time — which by the McGucken Equivalence (Proposition X.1) is the arrow of x₄'s advance — twistor space is chiral, and the googly problem of twistor theory is resolved as the geometric statement of x₄-irreversibility."* — [68, Proposition VII.1]

The physical association of the boundary z → 0 with the asymptotic limit of McGucken Sphere expansion follows [MG-AdSCFT §3.2 "Promoting to curved spacetime: the conformal boundary"] verbatim: *"the conformal boundary is a timelike hypersurface at spatial infinity. This boundary plays the same structural role that the McGucken Sphere plays in flat spacetime: it is where lightlike trajectories end, where global constraints are naturally expressed, and where the complete pattern of bulk correlations is encoded."*

*Motivation for the identification* (not a derivation from first principles; the identification z = L²/x₄ is the convention adopted in Theorem 15.2 below, motivated by two separate pieces of established physics):

(a) *Susskind–Witten UV/IR dictionary* (Susskind–Witten, "The Holographic Bound in Anti-de Sitter Space", arXiv:hep-th/9805114, abstract verbatim): *"the unusual aspect of the correspondence between Anti-de Sitter space and the boundary conformal field theory, which is that infrared effects in the bulk theory are reflected as ultraviolet effects in the boundary theory."* In AdS Poincaré coordinates, bulk excitations at radial depth z correspond to boundary CFT modes at UV scale μ with μ · z ~ L (in natural units ℏ = c = 1); the AdS radius L sets the crossover.

(b) *McGucken substrate-wavelength-to-energy correspondence* (Proposition 3.5 above, Compton-clock): a substrate x₄-oscillation mode of x₄-wavelength λ_x₄ carries energy E ~ ℏc/λ_x₄, so λ_x₄ ~ 1/μ for the dual boundary mode at scale μ.

Combining (a) and (b): z and λ_x₄ are both proportional to 1/μ, so z and λ_x₄ are proportional to each other. With L as the single AdS geometric scale and z = λ_x₄ = L at the crossover, the proportionality z · λ_x₄ = L² is one natural ansatz consistent with the crossover and dimensional analysis. If we further identify the x₄-coordinate value with the x₄-wavelength at the boundary (a convention on how boundary data is parameterized by x₄, not a theorem), the ansatz z = L²/x₄ follows. [MG-AdSCFT §4.1 "Bulk wave equations and boundary operator data"] and [MG-AdSCFT §4.2 "Conformal symmetry from the expansion of x_4"] develop the McGucken reading of the standard AdS/CFT dictionary narratively; this lemma states the specific coordinate identification used in Theorem 15.2. ∎

*Consequence for Theorem 15.2 (used in Steps 2, 3, 4, and Lemma 15.2.A below).* A codim-2 spacelike hypersurface Σ ⊂ M with ∂Σ = ∂A ⊂ ∂M is at fixed AdS-time t in the Poincaré chart iff it is at fixed t in the McGucken chart (t is preserved by z = L²/x₄; only the radial coordinate is transformed). A "fixed-z" surface in the AdS chart is a "fixed-x₄" surface in the McGucken chart (equivalent statements under z = L²/x₄), and such a surface is called "x₄-stationary" in the McGucken chart. The two families of anchored surfaces on ∂A — "fixed-t" (varying over all radial positions) and "x₄-stationary" (all points sharing the same x₄, equivalently the same z) — are different geometric families in general; Lemma 15.2.A below establishes that both families contain the area-extremal γ_A in the static case, and that x₄-flux-stationarity replaces x₄-stationarity in the covariant/HRT case. The coordinate bijection z = L²/x₄ is the geometric substrate on which both families are defined.

**Lemma 15.2.A (Coincidence of x₄-stationary anchored surfaces with the RT/HRT variational extrema).** *In the static asymptotically AdS bulk of Poincaré or global coordinates, the codim-2 spacelike surfaces anchored on ∂A at fixed t are in bijection with the codim-2 spacelike surfaces anchored on ∂A at fixed x₄ (by the identification z = L²/x₄ of Lemma 15.2.0 above, combined with the Killing symmetry ∂_t of the static AdS metric). The minimum-area surface γ_A in either family therefore coincides. In the covariant/HRT case (Hubeny–Rangamani–Takayanagi 2007, JHEP 07:062), where the bulk is dynamical and ∂_t is not Killing, the x₄-flux-stationary anchored surface replaces the fixed-x₄ minimum-area surface: γ_A is characterized as the extremal (saddle-point) surface of the area functional Area(Σ) = ∫_Σ √h  d^{d−1}x subject to ∂Σ = ∂A. In the McGucken Principle dx₄/dt = ic reading, x₄-flux-stationarity is the physical form of this extremal condition: γ_A is the anchored surface across which the x₄-flux from ∂A's Sphere-chain descendants is stationary against small variations of the surface embedding.*

*Proof.* Static case. The AdS Poincaré metric ds² = (L²/z²)(−dt² + dz² + dr² + r² dΩ²_{d−2}) has ∂_t as a Killing vector. Under the McGucken identification z = L²/x₄ (Lemma 15.2.0 above), a surface at fixed AdS-time t is a surface at fixed t in the McGucken chart (t is preserved by the identification), and a surface at fixed z is a surface at fixed x₄ in the McGucken chart. The induced metric on a codim-2 spacelike surface at fixed t depends only on the transverse spatial coordinates (z, r, Ω_{d−2}); the area functional Area(Σ) = ∫_Σ √h  d^{d−1}x has the same value in both coordinate readings. The variational problem is therefore the same in the two charts, and γ_A ∈ 𝒜(∂A) at fixed x₄ coincides with the standard Ryu–Takayanagi surface at fixed t.

Covariant case. The Hubeny–Rangamani–Takayanagi extremal surface satisfies δArea/δX^μ = 0 for variations δX^μ of the surface embedding subject to δX^μ|_{∂A} = 0. Σ is codim-2 spacelike, so its normal bundle at each point is 2-dimensional, spanned by one timelike and one spacelike direction. Pick the direction that is x₄-advancing: the unique unit vector n^μ_{x₄} in the normal bundle at p ∈ Σ that is x₄-forward (i.e., aligned with the +ic direction of dx₄/dt per Lemma 15.2.0 above) and null-rotation-adjusted so that n^μ_{x₄} coincides with the past Sphere-chain generator at p ∈ Σ. Under the McGucken Principle dx₄/dt = ic, the extremization is against variations that preserve the x₄-flux through Σ in this direction. The x₄-flux through Σ is Φ_{x₄}(Σ) = ∫_Σ n^μ_{x₄} √h  d^{d−1}x. By the mode-density identity of Step 2 above, Φ_{x₄}(Σ) is proportional to Area(Σ) with proportionality constant 1/ℓ_P² (one x₄-mode per Planck cell of transverse area). Therefore δΦ_{x₄}/δX^μ = (1/ℓ_P²) δArea/δX^μ, and the x₄-flux-stationarity condition δΦ_{x₄}/δX^μ = 0 is equivalent to the standard δArea/δX^μ = 0 extremality condition of Hubeny–Rangamani–Takayanagi. The McGucken Principle dx₄/dt = ic thereby recovers the covariant HRT extremal surface as the x₄-flux-stationary anchored surface. ∎
